# Ask Hava test design

Written in kit step K5 (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:599`; `sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:34`). It says what is tested, at which layer, against which requirement, and who writes and runs it. Tests are designed per **behavior, boundary, failure and invariant**, never one per requirement (stress-test F17, `sources/04_reviews/ChatGPT_Build_StressTest_Report.md:76`). Every requirement ID in `docs/spec/` appears in at least one entry below or, for A11Y, in the section 6 table (whose rows are the accessibility tests' IDs for rule 2: a test names its section 6 row by its first words, D-482). The twin's warnings and the hard-failing `Twin` check are in `docs/twin-warnings.md`. Decisions are D-101 to D-110 in `docs/decisions.md`; the round-2 simulation fixes changed or added entries under D-127 to D-162 (D-162 lists them); the business-side fix plan changed or added entries under D-172 to D-207; the three-angle fix plan changed or added entries under D-208 to D-267.

---

## 1. Rules for every test

1. **Assert what the outside world sees.** A test passes on what the fake carrier delivered, what Stripe's mock recorded, what the page shows, what the database and audit log hold; never on "function X was called". This is how the twin's checks work (`sources/01_spec/hhq_twin/README.md:53`) and why they catch bugs the app's own labels hide.
2. **Name the rule.** Each Rust test carries a doc comment `/// TD-FEE-01; FEE-001, ...` (and each Playwright or pytest test the same IDs in its title or marker); a docs test fails on a test file that names none. The test name says the case (`exactly_500_00_is_the_75_tier`), not the function (D-453).
3. **Boundaries both sides.** Every number in the spec is tested at, just under and just over the edge (amounts to the cent, times to the second).
4. **Failures are first-class.** Every outside call has at least one test where the vendor errors, times out, answers twice or answers late.
5. **Fake data only** (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:66`; PRIV-019). Fixtures come from one generator module: names from a fixed fake list, phones in the fictional `555-0100` to `555-0199` range under area codes in the quiet-hours table (for example `+1 928 555 0142`), emails at `example.com`, streets like "1 Test Way" (D-109). No real job text, photo or person is ever pasted in.
6. **Time is injected.** Tests use the `core` clock (`docs/architecture.md` section 5) and advance it; no test sleeps. Timers fire by running due tasks after advancing the clock.
7. **Deterministic in `Ci`.** proptest runs with a fixed seed in `Ci` and randomized with more cases in the nightly run; any failing case found at night is committed to the test's `proptest-regressions` file and kept (D-101, D-453).
8. **Tests are owned.** A test changes only with its owning task or through a spec change logged in `docs/decisions.md` (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:69`). A wrong expected value is fixed in the spec first, then the test.
9. **Reviewers judge assertions, not counts.** The fresh-eyes reviewer (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:249`) checks that each test would fail if the rule it names were broken. A test with no assertion on an outside effect, or that passes with the rule deleted, is sent back.

## 2. Layers

Per `BUILD_BRIEF.md:467`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:481`.

| Layer | Folder | What | Runs in | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Unit | `app/<module>/src/tests/<name>.rs` (in-crate, `#[cfg(test)]`, D-481) | Pure rules in each crate's `rules`: fees, the 4-month rule, eligibility, quiet hours, keyword matching, scrubber, state transitions | Every PR | `Ci` |
| Property | `app/<module>/src/tests/prop_<name>.rs` (in-crate, D-481) | proptest: invariants over generated inputs; a state-machine test for the job lifecycle | Every PR (derandomized); nightly randomized | `Ci` |
| Deliberate break (mutation) | `tests/mutation/` | Named mutants that must turn tests red (section 4), plus cargo-mutants | Named mutants: every PR touching their files and nightly; cargo-mutants: nightly | `Ci` |
| Integration | `tests/integration/tests/` | Real PostgreSQL 16; job queue with two workers; outbox and inbox never double-send; migrations; module boundaries | Every PR | `Ci` |
| Contract | `tests/contract/<vendor>/` | Each stand-in against the vendor's published API (request and response shapes, errors, webhook signatures); in Phase 2 the same tests against the sandbox (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:191`) | Every PR; Phase 2 with `--sandbox` | `Ci` |
| Twin | `twin/gate.py` | The twin as oracle; any non-waived invariant failure fails (`docs/twin-warnings.md` section 6). Until stage 1 links the twin to the app's rules, `Twin` checks only the stand-alone twin, not `app/`; until K8 builds the gate it is the informational smoke run | Every PR; nightly | `Twin` |
| End to end | `tests/e2e/*.spec.ts` | The four journeys (section 5) in Playwright for TypeScript against the app with stand-ins | Every PR | `Ci` |
| Security | `tests/security/tests/` | Authorization matrix, token isolation, brute force, webhook forgery, replay, headers, injection, compile-fail proofs (trybuild); plus gitleaks, the PII scanner, cargo-audit, cargo-deny and npm audit as `Ci` steps | Every PR | `Ci` |
| Accessibility | `tests/a11y/*.spec.ts` | axe-core (`@axe-core/playwright`) at 320, 768 and 1280px on every page and state; keyboard-only journeys; reflow, zoom, spacing (section 6) | Every PR | `Ally` |
| Documents | `tests/docs/` (pytest) | Scans of templates, pages and drafted documents for required and banned wording; terms-match-code checks | Every PR | `Ci` |
| Recovery | `tests/recovery/tests/` | Local restore and replay in Phase 1; the Railway drill with switchover in Phase 2 | Nightly; Phase 2 | Phase 2 |

The kit's own tests (`tests/test_trace_spec.py`, `tests/test_check_catalog.py`) stay at the top of `tests/`.

**Coverage** (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:481`): `Ci` measures branch coverage with cargo-llvm-cov (its branch mode runs on a pinned nightly toolchain, used for measuring only; builds and tests use the pinned stable release, D-453). Floors live in `tests/coverage_floor.json`: the total, and 95% for `app/payments`, `app/catalog` fee rules, `app/privacy` and `app/licensing` (FEE-034, PRIV-022, LIC-033). A PR may raise a floor; lowering one needs a decision.

## 3. Who designs, writes and runs

"Opus designs, Sonnet writes, Haiku runs" (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:34`; `BUILD_BRIEF.md:496`–`BUILD_BRIEF.md:498`).

| Work | Model |
|---|---|
| This design; adding entries when a spec line changes; matching worked examples (section 7.2); mutant lists; reviewing test PRs for money, privacy, licensing, security and recovery; triaging surviving mutants and new twin failures | **Opus** |
| Writing the tests inside each build task, **before** its code (`sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:271`); fixtures and generators; stand-in contract tests; named mutants for the code the task writes; Playwright journeys | **Sonnet** |
| Running suites, the twin gate, the nightly cargo-mutants and randomized proptest runs, axe scans; returning a short summary of failures to the session (`sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:189`) | **Haiku** |

K6 copies each entry's ID into the task that owns its module, so every build task names the TD entries its tests must cover.

## 4. Deliberate-break tests (mutation)

`BUILD_BRIEF.md:472` requires that breaking the 4-month rule or the license filter turns tests red; the Kit Plan makes it K5's done criterion (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:34`). K5 extends the same treatment to fee tiers, consent and opt-out, and quiet hours (D-108).

**Two mechanisms (D-108):**

- **Named mutants** (`tests/mutation/mutants.toml`, protected): each entry gives a file, an exact text replacement and the tests that must fail. `scripts/run_named_mutants.py` (T056) applies each one in place in the working tree with one shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, runs only the named test binaries (`cargo test -p <crate> -- <test>`), restores the file, and fails `Ci` if any mutant survives. On a PR it runs only the mutants of files that PR changed; nightly it runs the full list; a PR touching `app/payments/src/rules.rs` must finish the step in under 10 minutes (D-481). The task that writes the code writes its mutants; the list below is the minimum.
- **cargo-mutants** (nightly) on `app/payments/src/rules.rs`, `app/catalog/src/rules.rs`, `app/licensing/src/rules.rs`, `app/quotes/src/rules.rs` (matching), `app/messaging/src/rules.rs` (quiet hours, opt-out words) and `app/intake/src/rules.rs` (consent, cancel intent). `tests/mutation/mutants.toml` lists the files; the in-crate tests run against each mutant (D-481). Every surviving mutant is either killed by a new test or listed in `tests/mutation/equivalent.toml` with the reason, after an Opus review.

| Rule | Named mutants (each must turn a test red) | Killed by |
|---|---|---|
| **4-month rule** | multiplier 4→3; 4→5; 4→2 (the superseded rule); recurring quote charged at face value; weekly converted ×4 instead of ×52÷12; every-two-weeks ×2 instead of ×26÷12; yearly ÷4 instead of ÷12; a one-time job in a recurring category charged at 4 months (D-037); a period accepted in a non-recurring category; the $75 recurring cap removed, or applied to a one-time job (D-196) | TD-FEE-01, TD-FEE-03, TD-FEE-21 (W11 to W20, W30, W32, W33; REC-003's $150 → $600 → $75) |
| **Fee tiers** ($25 / $75 / $199) | `< 500` → `<= 500`; `<= 1500` → `< 1500`; any tier amount changed (including to a superseded fee); rounding down → rounding half up (D-033); range basis from the high or low end instead of the midpoint; trip-fee quote priced from its amount instead of the category's set tier; the AI "major" flag raising the tier; a repeat customer charged; a second free first win for the same entity key | TD-FEE-01, TD-FEE-02, TD-FEE-04, TD-FEE-08, TD-FEE-09 (W02 to W05, W08, W21, W22, W23, W24, W30, W31) |
| **License filter** | "covers **every** candidate job type" → "covers **any**"; the ticked-job-type check dropped; a sub-license treated as its full class (R-39R as R-39, LIC-035); `handyman_allowed` ignored; handyman limit `>= 1000` → `> 1000`; limit judged on the midpoint instead of the top; a card sent when the latest daily check is not Active; the license end date ignored; the gas keyword override removed (AI-012); a permit-type job allowed to a handyman; the license check at pick skipped (killed by TD-PICK-09, owned by T073, D-479); plain-keyword job types dropped after a trade answer (the twin's C1 bug, D-102) | TD-LIC-02, TD-LIC-05, TD-LIC-08, TD-LIC-09, TD-INT-03, TD-PICK-09; matching examples M01 to M16 |
| **Consent and opt-out** | one opt-out word removed from the list (each of the 11 in turn); sole-word match → contains-word ("can stop by at 3" opts out); an opt-out closing a job (D-005); bare "cancel" closing a job; the send path skipping the opt-out check; a second opt-out confirmation; a 21610 failure retried; a non-consent text allowed as the first message (other than the business-intent sign-up reply and the bare-HELP reply, D-131, D-132); a job card sent before consent; a consent word removed from the list (each of ok, okay, yes, y, yep, yeah, sure, sí, si in turn); first-word match → whole-message match ("yes please" ignored); the consent re-send dropped or sent twice; a sole-word CANCEL from a number with a live job not pausing the job; HELP after STOP not answered | TD-CON-01 to TD-CON-06, TD-CON-11, TD-CON-12, TD-JOB-05 |
| **Quiet hours** | end 20:00 → 21:00 (the superseded v9.3 window); start 08:00 → 07:00; only Arizona time checked; only the recipient's zone checked; an area code not in the table texted instead of refused (D-100, D-139); a non-urgent kind added to the night exceptions; an emergency job's quote texts held at night (D-136); the 15-minute direct-reply window widened; retries skipping the check (QH-008); 20:00:00 allowed | TD-QH-01, TD-QH-02 |
| **Card and summary gates** | a job card sent that the homeowner wasn't shown at INT-041; an AI quote summary shown while the "AI quote summaries" setting is off; a fee-free job type (FEE-045) charged a fee (D-493) | TD-AI-17, TD-PICK-29, TD-FEE-28 |

The twin's own rules get the same treatment inside the gate's self-tests (`docs/twin-warnings.md` section 6.5), because the current twin cannot see a broken license table.

## 5. The four end-to-end journeys

Playwright (TypeScript) against `web` and `worker` with every adapter on its stand-in, a real local PostgreSQL, and a test-only control router (advance the clock, read the fake carrier's delivered messages, read stripe-mock's records). The control router is compiled out of the release binary under the Cargo feature `test-control` that SEC-035 names and, where compiled in, accepts only the per-run bearer secret; TD-SEC-05 and TD-SEC-15 check it is absent in live configuration and in the release build (D-491). Each journey also runs keyboard-only and with axe at three widths (section 6), so the same steps feed `Ci` and `Ally` (A11Y-021). Every journey ends with the same **closing checks**: no duplicate outbound message or charge, the ledger reconciles to stripe-mock to the cent, every status change has an audit row, and no sentinel contact detail appears outside the vault (TD-PRIV-02).

**J1 Homeowner** (INT, CON, QH, CARD, PICK, CALL, JOB), in the INT-043 order (D-446).
1. A fake homeowner texts "AC blowing warm air" from `+1 928 555 0142`; that first text is the consent (CON-005); the 911 check finds nothing and sends no safety or heat text (INT-002, D-436); the first reply is the consent text (CON-031) and the next text follows with no reply asked for (D-419).
2. ZIP 86403 and the area check come first (INT-016, D-460); then both stand-in readings say electrical, the keyword rules say heating/AC, the trade question is asked and answered "2", the stored job type is `ac_repair` (TD-INT-03, the twin's six warnings as a regression), and the coverage line names the count of heating and cooling businesses in that town (INT-017) (D-482).
3. "How soon?" → 2 (INT-030); the hvac set's questions one at a time, the stand-in AI off (INT-039); two photos asked and one sent by picture text, checked on the server and shown back (INT-044); first name and address to the vault (INT-027); no own-or-rent question (D-437).
4. The card is shown by link; SEND sends it (INT-041); cards go to every eligible business (five seeded: R-39, R-39R that ticked AC repair, R-39 that ticked only heating, one paused, one in Kingman): exactly the first two get the card with the photo (CARD-001, CARD-013); the cards-out text (TXT-032) and no updates question; updates go by text (TXT-018, D-460).
5. Two diagnostic-visit quotes arrive (PICK-053); the first-quote text holds the free line, the LIST line and the link (TXT-016); the quote page shows both in arrival order with "Every eligible quote shown, in the order received." (WORD-005), license lines and review links (PICK-041), announced by `aria-live` (A11Y-014).
6. Pick quote 2 → review step with the share box unticked and required (PICK-049, D-492) and only "By picking, you agree to the Terms" beside the Pick button (PICK-016) → "Go back" → pick again → code: a wrong code, then the right one (PICK-013, PICK-017); contact choice: masked call (PICK-018), no call window and no who-lets-in question (D-440); focus moves to the result heading; the pick stores the terms acceptance.
7. The business confirms; stripe-mock succeeds; the webhook arrives twice (SEC-017); first name and address go to the business once, without the phone number (PICK-022, PICK-023); the business calls through the masked number for 50 seconds (CALL-007); day 3 "DONE" → Done → one private feedback request (JOB-017); the other quoter gets the PICK-026 "went to another business this time" notice once (D-482).

Variants: Spanish homeowner with Spanish conversations on (every text from the Spanish template, TXT-002, TXT-039); web chat start (CON-025); STOP after quotes arrive: no further texts, the job continues, the pick code goes by email (CON-012, PICK-029); 911-list words first ("I smell gas"): the INT-004 text, then the consent text, no card until "safe" (INT-005); "pipe burst" or "AC died, 92 inside": no safety text, the urgent choice instead (D-436).

**J2 Business** (BIZ, LIC, CON, FEE, PICK).
1. Sign-up for an R-37 plumbing business on a fixture ROC record; E-SIGN box starts unticked (CON-019); license class read from the record and read back (LIC-006, LIC-027).
2. Identity code to the record's phone through Verify (BIZ-002, TXT-038); with the code and license check passing and no flag, activation is automatic (BIZ-016); the Google and Yelp links and license number are saved (BIZ-052); the sign-up email is verified (BIZ-040); with the business-texting switch off, email is the only channel offered (BIZ-036); the welcome email goes before the first card (CON-020).
3. A card arrives by email (CARD-020) and the quote form opens from its link; a portal quote containing a phone number bounces (PICK-007); a portal firm quote of $650.00 for a faucet replacement, the job type's format, with a job note is accepted (PICK-053, PICK-052); the fake carrier records no text to any business phone (TXT-037).
4. The homeowner picks it and gets "Done. <Business> has your pick. We'll text you when they accept." (PICK-045); the pick email ("Accept by <time>", with "Can't take it") arrives (PICK-050), no call is placed to any business phone, and Confirm opens from the email (PICK-037, BIZ-039); Confirm shows a review step with the $75 fee and "Go back" (PICK-021, W04); card entry through the Stripe test element (keyboard only; the frame itself is a Gate B manual check, A11Y-022); authorization text and version stored (FEE-015).
5. Charged once; details appear in the portal; the statement CSV equals the ledger (BIZ-015, FEE-030).

Variants: with the business-texting switch on, a business that chose both gets the card and pick by text from the business number as well, never with homeowner details (TXT-035, TXT-036); a business that misses the pick email confirms in time after the 1-hour reminder email, and one that never confirms lapses and the homeowner gets the other quotes by text and picks with "1 YES" (PICK-037, PICK-046); a handyman quote of $1,000.00 is refused with the limit explanation (LIC-019, W26); a free first win records $0 with the waived $75 (FEE-012, W24); a business joining after day 90 has no free win (FEE-010).

**J3 Operator (Casey)** (SEC, OPS, FEE, LIC).
1. Owner sign-in with password and a test TOTP secret (SEC-005); a session over 12 hours is refused.
2. Pause the plumbing trade switch: a new plumbing request gets no card; unpause (OPS-003, CARD-016).
3. Refund a charge through the review step after a fresh TOTP (the SEC-043 step-up); one Stripe refund, one ledger row (FEE-026, FEE-028, D-491).
4. Switch fee mode to flat, with a TOTP entered within the last 5 minutes (SEC-043): the change is logged and the business must accept a new authorization before it can confirm (FEE-007, D-072, D-491).
5. Raise a High alert (the stand-in delivery rate below 95% for 30 minutes, OPS-004); advance 24 hours without acknowledging: safe mode pauses charges and releases while a gas-smell request still gets its safety text within 5 minutes and a STOP still gets its confirmation (OPS-001, INT-003, CON-009, D-133); the paused card timer does not run (FEE-038); a "Confirm and pay" press now is refused with "try again later" and stripe-mock records no charge; the owner session has ended after 30 minutes idle, so Casey signs in again (SEC-043); acknowledge: the paused release goes ahead after the FEE-017 check, and the business's next press charges once (OPS-024, FEE-016, D-491).
6. The audit log viewer shows every action above with its actor (SEC-006, OPS-010).

**J4 Vendor outage** (TXT, FEE, AI, OPS, WEB).
1. Fake Twilio starts failing sends: texts retry 3 times over 30 minutes, then the quote link goes by email and appears in the portal (TXT-014); with no email on file, Casey gets a call-back alert (TXT-015).
2. Fake Twilio reports the number suspended: all texting pauses; the site banner appears with `role="status"` (WEB-007, A11Y-018); a new request through web chat still works (WEB-009); no other number is used (TXT-022); Casey's call list fills (TXT-023); a "smell gas" web-chat message gets the safety text there and a critical alert (TXT-024).
3. stripe-mock is unreachable when the business confirms: per-attempt idempotency keys (FEE-016); after 4 hours the pick is released and the next quote offered (FEE-018); Stripe comes back and a late success arrives: refunded once, Casey told (FEE-021).
4. The stand-in AI is down: one "we have your message" text, retry every 30 minutes (INT-021); "sparks" in a message still gets the safety text within 5 minutes (INT-003).
5. Everything recovers; the closing checks pass, and the nightly reconcile finds nothing unmatched (FEE-029).

## 6. Accessibility checks

| Check | How | Covers | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| axe-core on every page and state (empty, filled, error, after pick, banner on, Spanish) at 320, 768 and 1280px; any violation fails | `tests/a11y/` injects the vendored axe-core 4.14.0 through Playwright; the page list is generated from the route table so a new page can't be skipped | A11Y-001, A11Y-002 | `Ally` |
| One `h1`, landmarks, skip link, `lang` on page and inline passages, ARIA only where needed | DOM assertions per page | A11Y-003, A11Y-004 | `Ally` |
| Keyboard-only journeys J1 to J4: tab order, visible focus not hidden behind the sticky header (focused element's box inside the viewport and not under the header), no traps | Playwright keyboard driver; focus-visible style asserted on each focused element | A11Y-005, A11Y-021 | `Ally` |
| Target size at least 24×24 CSS px; no drag-only control | Bounding boxes of every interactive element | A11Y-006 | `Ally` |
| Contrast | axe colour-contrast rule plus a token check of the CSS palette (4.5:1 text, 3:1 large text and UI) | A11Y-007 | `Ally` |
| Reflow at 320px and 400% zoom with no sideways scroll; data tables scroll in a labelled focusable region | `scrollWidth <= clientWidth` per page; table wrapper attributes | A11Y-008 | `Ally` |
| Text spacing override (WCAG 1.4.12 values) breaks nothing; `prefers-reduced-motion` stops motion | Inject the spacing stylesheet and re-check overflow and clipping; emulate reduced motion | A11Y-009 | `Ally` |
| Forms: visible labels, instructions before inputs, error summary linked and announced, nothing asked twice, paste and autofill allowed | DOM assertions; submit invalid forms; check `autocomplete` and that no `onpaste` blocks | A11Y-010, A11Y-011, A11Y-012, A11Y-013, PICK-014 | `Ally` |
| New quotes announced politely; focus to the result heading after a pick | `aria-live="polite"` region updates; `document.activeElement` | A11Y-014 | `Ally` |
| Time limits only on codes (pick, sign-in, Confirm and identity codes and the 5-minute fresh-code windows), sign-in links, owner sessions and the owner's 30-minute idle timeout; warning before expiry; one-step renew; entered data kept; quote windows as plain text (D-491) | Advance the clock to one minute before expiry; assert the warning and the renew control; no countdown element on the quote page | A11Y-015, A11Y-016 | `Ally` |
| Help in the same place on every page; status and alerts with the right role | Same landmark position across pages; role attributes | A11Y-017, A11Y-018 | `Ally` |
| Emails: real text, plain-text part, meaningful link text | Fake Resend payloads: a `text/plain` part exists; no image-only body; link text is not "click here" | A11Y-019 | `Ci` |
| Statement page content | Page assertions | A11Y-020 | `Ally` |
| Error prevention for money and agreements | Review steps with "Go back" before the final pick and Confirm | PICK-017, PICK-021 | `Ally` |
| Business channel settings, every email action page and the emails that link to them meet the rows above; link text names the action and the job; a plain-text part in every email; the Confirm window as an end time; every action, Hold included, shows what it will do first; no business notice needs a phone call or audio, and the emergency-email setup gives visual and vibration alert steps (D-333, D-344) | axe and DOM assertions over the settings and action pages at three widths; fake Resend payload checks | A11Y-023, A11Y-024 | `Ally` |
| Manual: VoiceOver on iPhone through the homeowner flow and the business card entry, including Stripe's frame, and opening a pick email and confirming or holding from it | Script in `docs/a11y.md`; Casey at Gate B; Claude records the result | A11Y-022 | Gate B |

## 7. Test catalog

Kind: **B** behavior, **E** boundary (edge), **F** failure, **I** invariant. Layer: U unit, P property, M mutation, In integration, C contract, E2E, S security, A accessibility, D documents, R recovery. "W##" are rows of `docs/catalog/worked_examples.yaml`; "M##" are the matching examples in section 7.2.

### 7.1 Fees and the 4-month rule (`payments`, `catalog`)

| ID | Kind | Layer | Covers | Cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TD-FEE-01 | E | U, P, M | FEE-001, FEE-002, FEE-008, FEE-034, REC-003 | Every W row is one parametrized test. Edges: 499.99 → $25, 500.00 → $75, 1,500.00 → $75, 1,500.01 → $199 (W02 to W05). Property: the fee is always 25, 75 or 199, never decreases as the basis rises, and the charged amount equals the tier exactly (no tax added, no percentage) |
| TD-FEE-02 | B | U, P | FEE-003, FEE-004, REC-005, PICK-053 | Range → midpoint (W08 to W10, W18); a diagnostic-visit quote → the category's set tier (W21, W22), and only in a `diagnostic_visit` category; fractions of a cent rounded down before the tier (W30, W31; D-033). Property: the basis of a range lies between its ends (D-441) |
| TD-FEE-03 | B | U, P, M, D | REC-001, REC-002, REC-004, REC-006, REC-010, REC-011 | Monthly ×4 (W11, W12); weekly ×52÷12 ×4 (W13); every two weeks (W14); quarterly (W15); yearly (W16, W17, W30); one-time job in a recurring category at face value (W32); a period in a non-recurring category is an error; a recurring-service quote with no period is bounced to the business; $40 per visit, weekly visits → $173.33 a month → basis $693.33 → $75 (D-159). Property: monthly *m* → basis is 4*m* rounded down to the cent. Document scan: no code, template, setting or copy uses another month multiplier for recurring services; W20 ($400 a month, basis $1,600.00) and W33 ($160 weekly, basis $2,773.33) pay $75 under the recurring cap (D-196) |
| TD-FEE-04 | B | U, In | FEE-005 | The tier is fixed at the pick from the chosen quote; the AI's major flag set or unset gives the same fee; a later edit to another quote changes nothing |
| TD-FEE-05 | I | In | FEE-006, JOB-016 | Charged at Confirm; a day-3 PROBLEM reply about a much higher final bill changes no ledger row |

### 7.2 Card eligibility and licensing (`quotes`, `licensing`, `catalog`)

**Matching worked examples (D-110).** The fee examples are an oracle the app didn't write (`sources/02_build_plan/Build_Plan.md:35`); matching needs the same, or the app and the twin can share one mistake (F07). An Opus task writes `docs/catalog/matching_examples.yaml` before any routing code, checked by `scripts/check_catalog.py`. The minimum set:

| M01 | hvac_repair, AC repair | R-39, ticked AC repair | Yes |
| M02 | hvac_repair, furnace repair | R-39R (AC only), ticked everything | No (LIC-035) |

### 7.5 Consent and opt-out (`intake`, `messaging`)

| ID | Kind | Layer | Covers | Cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TD-CON-01 | I | P, M, E2E | CON-001, CON-002, CON-003, CON-004, CON-026 | Property over every entry path (text and START return here; the web-chat path is TD-CON-10's, T103): the first outbound message to a new number is the consent text (the emergency path's safety text first is TD-CON-26's); the consent text holds every CON-002 phrase and the Terms and privacy links, and no fee line, "Reply OK" or "Reply YES" (D-419); the SMS terms' CON-003 fee line is TD-CON-27's; "HELP" as a new number's first message → the HELP reply, then the consent text when the person sends a request, and "I need help with my sink" as a first message → the consent text (moved from TD-CON-07); Spanish only when the first message reads as Spanish and Spanish conversations are on (TXT-039) (D-492) |
| TD-CON-02 | B | U | CON-005, TERMS-001, TERMS-016 | A new number's first text records consent at once (time, inbound message ID, channel "text", consent-text version) and no reply is asked for; the first intake question follows the consent text with no wait; "ok", "yes please", "Y" or "Sí" after the consent text are read only as intake answers, never as a consent event and never as confirming a pending pick; no Terms acceptance is stored until a pick (PICK-016) (D-419, D-420) |

### 7.6 Quiet hours (`messaging`)

| ID | Kind | Layer | Covers | Cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TD-QH-01 | E | U, P, M | QH-001, QH-002, QH-003, QH-009 | Allowed when the time is at least 08:00:00 and before 20:00:00 in both Arizona and the recipient's zone (07:59:59 no, 08:00:00 yes, 19:59:59 yes, 20:00:00 no; D-101). A Pacific number at 08:30 Arizona in January (07:30 Pacific) → held; the same in July → sent. An Eastern number at 18:00 Arizona in July (21:00 Eastern) → held. Both daylight-saving change days of the recipient's zone; Arizona never shifts. An area code not in the table is refused and never texted (D-100, D-139); a table test asserts every area code in the table has a zone. Property: allowed equals allowed-in-Arizona and allowed-in-zone |
| TD-QH-02 | B | U, In, M | QH-004, QH-005, QH-006, QH-007, QH-008, TXT-025 | Each night exception sent at 23:00 (safety, STOP/HELP/START replies, pick codes, a reply 14:59 after the person's text, emergency-job steps including its first-quote and update texts and the INT-029 questions after the cards, D-136, D-164); a reply exactly 15:00 or 15:01 after → held (D-101); a non-emergency job's first-quote text at 23:00 → held; job cards and confirm requests held for businesses, except emergency cards to after-hours businesses (D-054); a held text sent at 08:00:00; two update texts held overnight for one job → one merged text at 08:00 (D-158); dropped when the sending module's job-closed check (passed in by the caller, since `messaging` cannot read `jobs`) says the job closed meanwhile; retries obey the same rule (D-491) (D-493) |
| TD-QH-03 | B | U | BIZ-010 | A business's own quiet times narrow the window and can never widen it (D-085) |
| TD-QH-04 | E | U, In | QH-004, TXT-027 | On a job chosen as an emergency, at 23:10 the who's-calling text goes at once; the same text on a regular job is held to 08:00:00 (the missed-call text is TD-TXT-30's); the INT-033 texts go at night (D-270, D-271, D-491) |
| TD-QH-05 | E | In | QH-001, QH-006, QH-008, TXT-011, TXT-014, TXT-021, CON-009, CON-030 | In `tests/integration/tests/outbox_sender.rs` (T356, D-2628), through the worker with the stand-in Twilio: a `caller_missed` row queued at 21:30 Arizona time for a 928 number is not sent at 21:31 and is sent at 08:00; a consent text queued at 14:00 goes at once; a suppressed number gets only the HELP reply; a row queued while its line was suspended is expired, not sent, when the line reopens; a CON-009 confirmation goes within 5 minutes or never; a fake carrier that does not dedupe and a worker killed after the carrier accepted give one delivery and the row `unknown` after 5 minutes, never two; two workers racing for one row make one carrier call; a timeout gives `unknown`, the email copy of an important text and one Low alert |

### 7.9 Job lifecycle (`jobs`)

| ID | Kind | Layer | Covers | Cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TD-JOB-01 | I | P | JOB-001, JOB-002, JOB-003, JOB-005, JOB-006 | proptest state machine over quotes, picks, confirms, declines, calls, replies, cancels, closes and clock advances: one status at a time; only the arrows in `docs/architecture.md` section 6; an audit row per change; the timer cases are TD-JOB-03's (D-492) |
| TD-JOB-02 | I | In | JOB-004 | Two workers race a cancel and a Confirm, and a Stripe notice and a deadline, in both orders: one wins, the other applies to nothing (the twin's `races` scenario as oracle) |
| TD-JOB-03 | E | U, In | JOB-003, JOB-006, JOB-007, JOB-008, JOB-009, JOB-010, JOB-011, JOB-012, JOB-013, JOB-014, JOB-015, JOB-025, JOB-026, TXT-042 | When the last card's 24-hour window ends with quotes and no pick, exactly one "All quotes are in for request <n>: <k> of <N> businesses quoted." text, held to 8 a.m. in quiet hours and never after a pick or CLOSE; the 3-day close sends "Request <n> is closed because no quote was picked." (D-461). Business clock: a pick at 7:30 p.m. has its 4 business hours end at 11:30 a.m. (PICK-019, D-482); Open → No quotes yet at 6:00 p.m. next day with the text; 5 days → Closed; 3 days restarted on every return to Quotes ready; the failed quote can't be chosen again; a failed pick with no choosable quote left while another card's 24-hour window is open → the job waits in Quotes ready and a later quote is accepted, and closes only once every window has ended (D-140); the after-release contact cases are TD-JOB-07's; no reply 7 days after the day-14 check-in → Closed with no review request; every non-final status has a pending timer and a timer firing after the job moved does nothing (moved from TD-JOB-01); Intake → Open only after consent, questions, safety, area and duplicate checks (D-491) (D-492) |
| TD-JOB-04 | B | In | JOB-016, WEB-028, PICK-054 | Every day-3 PROBLEM is flagged to Casey with its text; the review request, the feedback page and the display rules are TD-JOB-10's (T230); the home page shows no numbers at 49 requests sent and the three computed numbers with their date range and trade count at 50 (D-468); the quote page and accepted-quote email offer the contact card, the after-Done page holds the "Know someone" line, and no text carries either (D-466, D-492, D-493) |
| TD-JOB-10 | B | In, E2E | JOB-017 | Split from TD-JOB-04: the review request goes only after Done, asking for private feedback; the feedback page's share box is unticked, only ticked feedback reaches the site, and a ticked negative one is shown like a positive one; a ticked one is left off only with one of the four allowed reasons, logged; feedback from the owner, a relative of the owner or a business is never shown; no page says the feedback shown is all the feedback received; the page passes axe (D-493) |
| TD-JOB-11 | B | In | JOB-009, JOB-011, TXT-016 | The first confirmed quote moves an Open or No quotes yet job to Quotes ready in the quote's own transaction with one `first_quote` homeowner-text event; a second quote, an edit, an AI-read quote before its confirm, and a quote in Quotes ready or Awaiting confirm move nothing and queue no second text; the old Open or No-quotes-yet wait applies nothing after the move and a stale no-quotes-yet text is not sent; the 3-day close counts from the move and a restart moves it; two first quotes at once make one move and one text; "2 YES" is taken after two quotes with no manual move (D-1517) |
| TD-JOB-12 | F | In, S | JOB-015, JOB-016, TXT-029, TXT-020, PRIV-006, FEE-035 | In `tests/integration/tests/check_in_replies.rs` (T277, D-1840), fictional texts through the real inbound route: a day-3 PROBLEM reply on a Connected job closes it, stores its scrubbed words in `privacy.message_body` bound to that job and inbox row before the route answers 200, and after a worker restart the owner-only `problem_words` read returns the words for the alert's inbox row; a stated name or phone in the reply is `[removed]` in the stored words; a business session and a homeowner session have no read; a database failure before the store rolls the delivery back so Twilio resends; a safety word gives the safety alert kind; a day-3 NO under email contact enqueues one `picks.unreachable_step` for the pick, due now (a repeat enqueues nothing more), and records no no-show itself; a reply on a job with no check-in due goes to intake as before; rows older than 30 days are deleted by the daily step |
| TD-JOB-13 | B | In | JOB-015, JOB-016, JOB-017, JOB-027, TXT-029, TXT-040 | In `tests/integration/tests/check_in_texts.rs` (T278, D-1842): a release that connects a job schedules its day-3 and day-14 check-ins from the later of the release and the earliest visit; the check-in, feedback, problem-reply, unreachable-closed and emailed-question templates render in English and Spanish and the sender sends each (none refused as a bad payload); the unreachable close queues `unreachable_closed` once in the close's transaction; TD-TXT-25's scan and TD-CON-24's guard pass unchanged |
| TD-JOB-14 | B | In | FEE-022, FEE-035, FEE-052, JOB-013, JOB-027, TXT-026, TXT-030 | In `tests/integration/tests/unreachable_steps.rs` (T281, D-1845), clock advanced, no sleeps: a release enqueues the steps at + 24, + 48, + 72 and + 96 hours once each; the reminders go at 24 and 48 hours unless a business call came first; the first unanswered business call queues the missed-call text at 8 a.m. Arizona time for a regular job and at once for an emergency; a number that can't be texted gets the TXT-030 email; the + 72 hours step returns a call-contact job with no call and sends the emailed question for email contact; a `RefundWaiting` step runs again an hour later and the decision happens once; a `picks.fraud_warning` task for an unreleased paid pick refunds, returns the job to Quotes ready and ends the pick once the refund has succeeded, runs again an hour later while the refund is in flight, and changes nothing for a released pick; an email-contact day-3 NO step from T277 records the no-show; a 96-hour run that fails after the close is retried and queues the decision email once (D-2204) |
| TD-JOB-15 | I | U | JOB-002 | `a_refused_or_stale_move_changes_nothing` (`app/jobs/src/tests/transitions.rs`, T289, D-1873) still asserts exactly one ERROR event for the refused move, every other assertion unchanged, and passes 200 runs in a row together with the crate's other tests, however they are scheduled |

### 7.13 Wording, website and terms (`site`, documents)

### 7.14 Security (`portals`, `adapters`, `core`)

| ID | Kind | Layer | Covers | Cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TD-SEC-01 | I | S | SEC-004, SEC-005, SEC-006, SEC-008, SEC-019, SEC-034 | Every route that shows contact details or photos answers with `Cache-Control: no-store` and no third-party script (D-454); authorization matrix generated from the route table: every route × anonymous, homeowner A, homeowner B, business X, business Y, owner → expected allow or deny; homeowner A's token never reads B's job; only the owner has pause, refund and release; sign-in rules per portal (link single use and 15 minutes, new-device code, 30-day sessions, owner 12 hours) |
| TD-SEC-02 | F | S | SEC-014, SEC-015, SEC-020 | 5 new requests per phone per hour accepted, the 6th refused; 100 fake requests in an hour blocked and alerted; brute force on pick codes, sign-in links and owner sign-in stops at the limits |
| TD-SEC-03 | F | S, C | SEC-016, SEC-017, SEC-018 | Forged Twilio or Stripe signature → 403 and a log line; Stripe timestamp 300 seconds old accepted, 301 refused; one event 100 times → one effect; every adapter method that acts outside requires an idempotency key (architecture test) |
| TD-SEC-04 | B | S | SEC-009, SEC-011, SEC-012, SEC-013 | Cookies `__Host-`, `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, rotated on login (D-099); a form post without the CSRF token → 403; homeowner text `<script>` shows as text; every response's headers; no third-party script or font host on any page except Stripe.js from js.stripe.com on the Confirm page, which Stripe Elements needs (FEE-014); the HSTS header has no `preload` (D-491, D-492) |
| TD-SEC-05 | B | U, S | SEC-001, SEC-002, SEC-003, SEC-010, SEC-021 | A live key in a test environment (and the reverse) stops startup; the test control router is absent when `APP_ENV=live`; every route input is a serde struct with `deny_unknown_fields` (architecture test); gitleaks, cargo-audit, cargo-deny and npm audit steps fail on a hit; `Cargo.lock` and `package-lock.json` are committed and the base image pinned by digest |

### 7.15 Accessibility

Section 6 covers every accessibility requirement: A11Y-001, A11Y-002, A11Y-003, A11Y-004, A11Y-005, A11Y-006, A11Y-007, A11Y-008, A11Y-009, A11Y-010, A11Y-011, A11Y-012, A11Y-013, A11Y-014, A11Y-015, A11Y-016, A11Y-017, A11Y-018, A11Y-019, A11Y-020, A11Y-021, A11Y-022, A11Y-023, A11Y-024.

| ID | Kind | Layer | Covers | Cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TD-A11Y-01 | B | A, E2E | A11Y-025, A11Y-026, A11Y-027, A11Y-028, A11Y-029 | The Ally report lists every incomplete axe result and fails on an unreviewed one; tabbing every page never leaves the focused element fully under a sticky bar; every photo upload is a native file input; every input border on paper is at least 3:1 and the pair test covers borders and the focus ring; every field has a visible label and no placeholder-as-label |
| TD-A11Y-02 | B | A, E2E, U | A11Y-030, A11Y-031, A11Y-032, A11Y-033, A11Y-034, A11Y-035 | With JavaScript off, a failed submit returns a page titled "Error: …" whose error summary is first in main and focused, with values kept; no submit button is ever disabled and a double submit charges or creates nothing twice; every code field accepts a pasted code and carries the one-time-code autocomplete; the quote page's status region exists empty at first render and announces one line per batch; every HTML email's root has lang; the web chat completes as one question per page without JavaScript |
| TD-A11Y-03 | I | A, D | A11Y-002 | After T267's split: the Ally run covers exactly the (route, state) pairs it covered on `main` before the split (listed by the run), each seeded by its own route's seed function; `tests/a11y/fixtures.ts`'s `WCAG_TAGS`, `REVIEWED_INCOMPLETE`, `NOT_PAGES`, `readRouteTable` and `axeScan` are unchanged; a GET route with no `PAGE_STATES` entry in `tests/a11y/page_states.ts` still fails `axe_all_pages.spec.ts`; `check_pr_change.py` lets a page-adding task's branch change `tests/a11y/page_states.ts` (a shared path since D-1788) (D-1772, D-1788) |

## 8. Order of work and done criteria

- **K6** turns each TD entry into test work inside the build task that owns the module, and adds two tasks before any routing code: the matching examples file (section 7.2) and the catalog's per-job-type license scope (K3 follow-up 1).
- **K8** builds the runner for named mutants, the cargo-mutants nightly job, the coverage floors file, the test control router guard and the Twin gate (`docs/twin-warnings.md` section 6.3).
- **Stage 1** (the twin task, a `harness` task with an Opus review because all of `twin/` is protected) fixes the intake causes C1 to C2e, re-measures the class-waiver ceilings and empties the pinned waivers.
- A build task is done only when its TD entries have tests carrying their markers, its named mutants are killed, coverage floors hold, and `Ci`, `Twin` and `Ally` pass (`BUILD_BRIEF.md:261`).
- **K5's own criterion** (`sources/02_build_plan/Kit_Plan_Before_Coding.md:34`): breaking the license filter or the 4-month rule turns tests red. Until the app exists this is shown on the existing code: `tests/test_check_catalog.py` already fails on a broken multiplier and a wrong tier (D-036), and the twin's broken sub-license was shown invisible to the current twin (`docs/twin-warnings.md` section 2), which the gate's self-tests will catch.
